- 21–22.5s: Nagumo thrusts an arm toward the window
- 22.5–25s: he snaps it out; two Zeros sweep past beyond the glass
Acting: his voice is strong but his jaw never relaxes.
Dialogue (NAGUMO, Japanese, forceful, cutting off) at 22.5–25s: "戦闘機がいるだろう！"
Camera: 50mm, short pan along his arm to the window.
Light: silhouette against the window.
Sound: the shout, Zero engines.
Ends on: the pointing arm held; tension in his jaw.

Shot 6 (25–30s) · CUT TO — EXECUTE
Refs: @image3, @image4, @image1
Frame: The STAFF OFFICER walks to the signal station; NAGUMO stays at the table; the officer's shoulder passes close to camera.
Action:
- 25–27s: the officer gives a short bow and heads for the signal station
- 27–28.5s: the signalman lifts the handset and takes it down
- 28.5–30s: the officer's shoulder fills the lens completely — cut
Camera: 35mm, following the officer, ending on a shoulder wipe.
Light: window light, dial lamps.
Sound: footsteps, radio static.
Ends on: the frame blacked by the shoulder.

[AUDIO]
Speak only the quoted lines, once each, in the stated language, at the stated moment. Diegetic sound only — wind, sea, engines, guns, metal, wordless voices — carried across cuts. No music, no narration.

C11 · 편집 위치 05:54–06:24

격납고 · 폭탄을 내린다

30초 · 6샷 · 첨부 6장 · 대사 0

명령 직후 격납고. 무장 반장의 호루라기에 수십 명이 달려든다. 육상 폭탄을 받쳐 내리고, 탄약고로 돌려보낼 시간이 없어 벽 옆에 쌓는다. 혼잡하고 덥고 쉬지 않는다.

프롬프트는 영어(시댄스용) · 샷 카드는 한국어(검토용) · 둘은 같은 내용

앞에서 받는 상황
C10의 어깨 와이프에서 이어진다(보고 장면 없음).

다음으로 넘기는 상황
격납고 전체가 움직이고 있다. 벽 옆에 쌓인 폭탄 더미 위로 작업등이 흔들린다.

첫 화면
격납고 와이드. 날개를 맞댄 B5N 줄, 배 아래 육상 폭탄. 낮은 강철 천장, 흔들리는 작업등, 열기의 아지랑이. 무장 반장이 호루라기를 문다.

번호순 첨부 이미지 6장 · 기존 에셋 그대로

모두 참고 이미지로 첨부한다. 시작·종료 프레임 지정 없음. 아래 기존 경로의 파일을 이 번호 순서로 올린다.

샷별 연출 6개 · 초 단위 동작
샷 010–5초 · 5초 · WHISTLE — the hangar moves

구도 격납고 와이드. 전경 왼쪽: B5N 날개 끝(흐림). 중경 가운데: 호루라기를 문 무장 반장. 배경: 줄지은 B5N, 낮은 천장의 보, 작업등.

  1. 0–1.5초 반장이 호루라기를 길게 분다.
  2. 1.5–3.5초 통로 양쪽에서 수십 명이 쏟아져 나와 기체 아래로 흩어진다.
  3. 3.5–5초 반장이 팔을 휘둘러 작업조들을 각 기체로 보낸다(단어 없는 고함).

카메라 24mm, 반장 뒤에서 앞으로 빠른 푸시 인.

빛 노란 작업등의 낮은 빛, 땀의 광택, 아지랑이.

소리 호루라기, 발소리, 고함, 위층의 막힌 포성.

작업 흐름 좌→우.

마지막 프레임 기체마다 붙은 작업조.

참고 @image1 · @image2 · @image6

샷 025–10초 · 5초 · CRADLE — take the weight

구도 B5N 배 아래 낮은 위치. 육상 폭탄(@image3의 폭탄) 바로 아래로 운반대(@image5)가 밀려 들어온다. 작업원 셋.

  1. 5–7초 둘이 운반대를 폭탄 아래 정확히 맞춘다.
  2. 7–8.5초 한 명이 잭 손잡이를 펌프질해 받침을 올린다.
  3. 8.5–10초 받침이 폭탄 배에 닿고 무게를 받아 운반대 바퀴가 눌린다.

카메라 35mm, 바닥 높이, 미세한 핸드헬드.

빛 기체 그늘 속 작업등 반사.

소리 잭 펌프 소리, 금속 맞닿음, 거친 숨.

작업 좌→우.

마지막 프레임 무게를 받은 받침.

참고 @image2 · @image3 · @image5 · @image6

샷 0310–14초 · 4초 · RELEASE THE SHACKLE

구도 인서트. 폭탄 걸쇠와 렌치를 돌리는 장갑 낀 손. 이마에서 떨어지는 땀방울.

  1. 10–12초 렌치가 세 번 돌아간다. 땀방울이 폭탄 위에 떨어진다.
  2. 12–14초 걸쇠가 풀리고 폭탄이 받침 위로 2cm 툭 내려앉는다.

카메라 100mm 매크로, 락 오프.

빛 작업등의 강한 하이라이트.

소리 렌치 래칫, 쿵.

—

마지막 프레임 빈 걸쇠.

샷 0414–19초 · 5초 · OUT — the empty rack

구도 통로 교차점. 오른쪽으로 빠지는 폭탄 운반대, 왼쪽에서 들어오는 어뢰 운반대(@image4). 뒤의 B5N에는 빈 거치대.

  1. 14–16초 넷이 몸을 숙여 폭탄 운반대를 오른쪽으로 민다.
  2. 16–17.5초 교차점에서 어뢰 운반대와 부딪힐 뻔해 둘 다 급정거한다.
  3. 17.5–19초 반장이 손짓으로 한쪽을 세우고 다른 쪽을 보낸다.

카메라 28mm, 교차점 높은 위치에서 느린 아크.

빛 교차하는 작업등 빛, 바닥의 기름 반사.

소리 바퀴 굴러가는 소리, 급정거, 호루라기 짧게.

반출 좌→우.

샷 0519–24초 · 5초 · NO TIME — stack them here

구도 격납고 벽면. 탄약고 승강기 옆이 아니라 벽 옆에 폭탄들이 쌓이기 시작한다. 그 옆에 연료 호스.

  1. 19–21초 작업원이 탄약고 쪽을 가리키자 반장이 고개를 젓고 벽을 가리킨다.
  2. 21–23초 폭탄이 벽 옆에 하나, 둘 쌓인다. 연료 호스가 그 옆을 지난다.
  3. 23–24초 반장이 이마의 땀을 훔치며 다음 기체로 달린다.

효과·물리 불길한 복선: 화면은 이를 강조하지 않고 그냥 보여준다.

카메라 50mm, 벽을 따라 느린 트래킹.

빛 벽에 드리운 짙은 그림자, 작업등의 광택.

소리 쇠 받침이 바닥을 긁는 소리, 숨, 위층 포성.

작업 좌→우.

마지막 프레임 연료 호스 옆에 쌓인 폭탄.

샷 0624–30초 · 6초 · EVERYWHERE AT ONCE

구도 크레인처럼 상승하는 와이드. 여러 작업조가 동시에 같은 일을 한다. 전경에 강철 보가 지나간다.

  1. 24–26초 카메라가 보 사이로 올라가며 격납고 전체가 드러난다.
  2. 26–28초 왼쪽에서는 폭탄이 나가고 오른쪽에서는 어뢰가 들어온다.
  3. 28–30초 배가 기울며 작업등들이 일제히 흔들리고 그림자가 휘청인다.

카메라 상승하는 크레인 무브, 보 와이프.

빛 흔들리는 작업등, 움직이는 그림자.

소리 수십 명의 소음, 바퀴, 위층의 막힌 포성.

좌→우 흐름.

마지막 프레임 흔들리는 작업등 아래의 혼잡.

이 클립의 얼굴 고정 (인물 바이블 발췌)
  • 격납고 무장 반장 — 35세. 넓고 평평한 얼굴, 땀 맺힌 삭발, 굵은 목, 머리띠처럼 감은 수건, 목에 건 호루라기.
  • 격납고 무장 작업원들 — 18–25세 수병들. 삭발, 땀에 젖은 흰·회색 작업복, 면장갑, 머리띠·수건. 같은 얼굴 반복 금지.
동일 인물·무장·손상·공간 상태

육상 폭탄 → 받침 → 분리 → 빈 거치대 → 반출 → 벽 옆 적재. 한 기체에 두 무장이 겹치지 않는다. 여러 작업조가 동시에.

전체 생성 프롬프트 펼치기 (7,716자)
[STYLE LOCK]
Photoreal live-action WWII epic, Battle of Midway, June 4 1942. Large-format cinema camera, spherical primes, fine film grain, soft highlight roll-off, deep atmospheric haze at distance.
Palette: steel-teal sea, milky high-key sky with towering cumulus, white coral sand, olive-drab, khaki and dark navy, warm tanned skin. Orange fire only at the instant of an explosion or a burning wing; flak is black, smoke charcoal, sea spray white. No oversaturated cyan, no game sheen.
Every frame in three layers: dark foreground occluder (sandbag, barrel, window frame, wing edge, hull rail) — subject — vast sky, sea or battle beyond. Exteriors: men small against the sky. Interiors: hard window light, haze, and the battle outside always visible or audible.
Real-time speed, no slow motion. 16:9, 1080p. Light, haze, smoke and wind stay continuous across cuts.

[TIME & PLACE]
Interior hangar: low hard work lamps, sweat-sheened skin, heat haze, deep shadows between aircraft; muffled AA through the deck above.

[IMAGES — all references, none as start/end frame]
@image1 [BG] Japanese carrier steel hangar: beams, passages, deck plates
@image2 [ASSET] B5N2 parked: wings spread, wheels down
@image3 [ASSET] B5N2 three-seat level bomber with land bomb
@image4 [ASSET] Japanese aerial torpedo on a wheeled cradle
@image5 [ASSET] Japanese ordnance carts, chocks, straps, tools
@image6 [ASSET] Japanese ordnance crew work clothes, cap, gloves

[REFERENCE LOCK]
BG images: copy layout, materials and geography only — not their camera, light or colour.
Aircraft/ship/weapon images: exact silhouette, structure, paint, markings, number of engines, barrels and funnels; never morph types.
Costume sheets: uniform and gear only, worn by living men with the CAST LOCK faces. Never show sheets, labels, mannequins or multiple views.
US insignia 1942: white star in blue circle, no red centre, no side bars. Japanese: red hinomaru.

[CAST LOCK — same faces in every shot]
ORDNANCE CHIEF — Japanese petty officer, 35. Broad flat face, shaved head glistening with sweat, thick neck, towel twisted into a headband, loud whistle on a cord.
ORDNANCE CREW — Japanese sailors, 18–25, shaved heads, white or grey work clothes darkened by sweat, cotton gloves, hachimaki or towels; every face different.
Japanese: authentic adult Japanese men of 1942 — lean, wiry, compact builds; enlisted heads shaved or near-shaved, officers short-cropped; monolid or hooded eyes, straight lower nose bridge, defined cheekbones, bronze tan, clean-shaven unless stated. Natural, dignified — never caricature, never modern idol styling.
Every other extra has a clearly different face; no clones.

[DIRECTION & PHYSICS]
American flight paths, ship headings, muzzles and profiles read RIGHT-TO-LEFT; Japanese read LEFT-TO-RIGHT — by camera placement, never by mirroring.
Propellers are blurred discs; aircraft keep exact shape; torpedoes fall nose-slightly-down, splash, then run straight leaving a bubble wake; bombs keep forward momentum; recoil, casings, spray and debris obey gravity. A destroyed aircraft never reappears. The camera never passes through glass, walls or hulls.

[NEGATIVE]
No on-screen text, subtitles, timestamps, logos, watermarks, letterbox. No music, no narration. No modern objects. No anime/CG/plastic look, no waxy skin, no cloned faces, no extra fingers or limbs.
One aircraft never carries a bomb and a torpedo at the same time. Bombs and torpedoes are never thrown. No calm moment.

[STRUCTURE]
30s, 6 shots, hard cuts. Timings are clip time.
First frame: hangar wide: rows of B5N2s wingtip to wingtip, land bombs under their bellies; low steel ceiling, swinging work lamps, heat haze; the ORDNANCE CHIEF puts a whistle to his lips.

[SHOTS]
Shot 1 (0–5s) — WHISTLE — the hangar moves
Refs: @image1, @image2, @image6
Frame: Hangar wide. Foreground left: a B5N2 wingtip, soft. Midground centre: the ORDNANCE CHIEF with the whistle. Background: rows of B5N2s, low ceiling beams, work lamps.
Action:
- 0–1.5s: the chief blows a long blast
- 1.5–3.5s: dozens of men pour from both sides of the passage and scatter under the aircraft
- 3.5–5s: the chief swings his arm, sending crews to each aircraft — wordless shouting
Camera: 24mm, fast push-in from behind the chief.
Light: low yellow work-lamp light, sweat sheen, heat haze.
Sound: whistle, running feet, shouts, muffled guns above.
Ends on: a crew at every aircraft.

Shot 2 (5–10s) · CUT TO — CRADLE — take the weight
Refs: @image2, @image3, @image5, @image6
Frame: Low under a B5N2's belly: a bomb cart (@image5) rolls in directly beneath the land bomb (as in @image3); three crewmen.
Action:
- 5–7s: two men line the cart up exactly under the bomb
- 7–8.5s: one pumps the jack handle, raising the cradle
- 8.5–10s: the cradle meets the bomb and takes its weight; the cart's tyres squash
Camera: 35mm at floor level, slight handheld.
Light: lamp glints in the shadow under the aircraft.
Sound: jack pumping, metal contact, hard breathing.
Ends on: the cradle carrying the weight.

Shot 3 (10–14s) · CUT TO — RELEASE THE SHACKLE
Refs: @image2, @image5
Frame: Insert: the bomb shackle and a gloved hand turning a wrench; a drop of sweat falling from a brow.
Action:
- 10–12s: the wrench turns three times; a sweat drop lands on the bomb
- 12–14s: the shackle opens; the bomb drops 2cm onto the cradle with a thud
Camera: 100mm macro, locked-off.
Light: hard lamp highlights.
Sound: wrench ratchet, the thud.
Ends on: the empty shackle.

Shot 4 (14–19s) · CUT TO — OUT — the empty rack
Refs: @image2, @image5, @image4, @image6
Frame: A passage intersection: the bomb cart heading out right, a torpedo cradle (@image4) coming in from the left; behind, the B5N2 with its empty rack.
Action:
- 14–16s: four men lean into the bomb cart, pushing it right
- 16–17.5s: at the intersection it nearly collides with the torpedo cradle; both jerk to a stop
- 17.5–19s: the chief signals one to hold and waves the other through
Camera: 28mm, slow arc from high above the intersection.
Light: crossing lamp light, oil sheen on the deck plates.
Sound: rolling wheels, a sudden stop, a short whistle.
Ends on: the B5N2 showing its empty rack.

Shot 5 (19–24s) · CUT TO — NO TIME — stack them here
Refs: @image1, @image5, @image6
Frame: Hangar bulkhead: bombs begin piling beside the wall, not sent down to the magazine; fuel hoses run alongside.
Action:
- 19–21s: a crewman points toward the magazine lift; the chief shakes his head and jabs at the wall
- 21–23s: bombs are set down by the wall, one, two; a fuel hose runs right beside them
- 23–24s: the chief wipes his brow and runs to the next aircraft
Camera: 50mm, slow tracking along the bulkhead.
Light: deep shadows on the bulkhead, lamp gleam on the bombs.
FX: an ominous detail — shown plainly, not emphasised.
Sound: steel scraping the deck, breathing, guns above.
Ends on: bombs stacked beside the fuel hose.

Shot 6 (24–30s) · CUT TO — EVERYWHERE AT ONCE
Refs: @image1, @image2, @image6, @image4
Frame: A rising crane-like wide: several crews doing the same job at once; a steel beam passes through the foreground.
Action:
- 24–26s: the camera rises between beams; the whole hangar is revealed
- 26–28s: on the left bombs go out; on the right torpedoes come in
- 28–30s: the ship heels; every lamp swings at once; shadows lurch
Camera: rising crane move with a beam wipe.
Light: swinging lamps, moving shadows.
Sound: dozens of men, wheels, muffled gunfire above.
Ends on: the crush under swinging lamps.

[AUDIO]
Speak only the quoted lines, once each, in the stated language, at the stated moment. Diegetic sound only — wind, sea, engines, guns, metal, wordless voices — carried across cuts. No music, no narration.

C12 · 편집 위치 06:24–06:50

격납고 · 어뢰를 올린다

26초 · 5샷 · 첨부 6장 · 대사 0

어뢰 운반대가 밀려 들어와 비워진 거치대에 맞춰진다. 바깥 공격에 배가 휘청여도 손은 멈추지 않는다. 일은 끝나지 않았고, 위층의 대공 사격은 점점 거세진다.

프롬프트는 영어(시댄스용) · 샷 카드는 한국어(검토용) · 둘은 같은 내용

앞에서 받는 상황
C11의 흔들리는 작업등과 혼잡을 잇는다.

다음으로 넘기는 상황
반쯤 바뀐 줄의 기체들, 쌓인 폭탄, 거세지는 위층 포성. 작업등이 화면을 가로지르며 컷.

첫 화면
격납고 바닥 높이. 네 명이 긴 어뢰를 실은 운반대를 화면 왼쪽에서 밀고 들어온다. 은빛 몸체에 작업등이 흐른다.

샷별 연출 5개 · 초 단위 동작
샷 010–5초 · 5초 · TORPEDO IN

구도 바닥 높이. 전경: 바닥 철판 이음매. 중경: 어뢰 운반대(@image4)와 미는 네 명. 배경: B5N의 빈 거치대.

  1. 0–2초 네 명이 운반대를 민다. 어뢰가 작업등 빛을 받아 흐른다.
  2. 2–3.5초 앞바퀴가 철판 이음매에 걸려 운반대가 멈칫한다.
  3. 3.5–5초 다 함께 힘을 주자 바퀴가 넘어가고 어뢰가 받침 위에서 한 번 출렁인다. 손들이 즉시 붙잡는다.

카메라 24mm 바닥 높이, 운반대와 함께 뒤로 이동.

빛 어뢰 몸체의 하이라이트, 땀.

소리 바퀴, 쿵, 숨, 단어 없는 구령.

운반 좌→우.

마지막 프레임 빈 거치대 아래 도착한 어뢰.

참고 @image4 · @image1 · @image6

샷 025–10초 · 5초 · ALIGN

구도 B5N 배 아래. 앞쪽 작업원이 어뢰 머리를, 뒤쪽이 꼬리 핀을 잡고 맞춘다.

  1. 5–7초 잭이 어뢰를 천천히 들어 올린다.
  2. 7–9초 꼬리 핀이 동체에 닿을 듯하자 뒤 작업원이 손바닥으로 비틀어 맞춘다.
  3. 9–10초 어뢰가 거치대 바로 아래서 멈춘다.

카메라 35mm, 동체 아래 낮은 위치, 상승을 따라 아주 느린 틸트 업.

빛 기체 그늘, 은빛 어뢰.

소리 잭, 금속 스침.

샷 0310–14초 · 4초 · LOCK

구도 인서트. 걸쇠가 닫히고 고정 띠가 조여진다. 반장의 손바닥이 어뢰를 두드린다.

  1. 10–11.5초 걸쇠가 딸깍 닫힌다.
  2. 11.5–13초 고정 띠가 두 번 당겨져 조여진다.
  3. 13–14초 반장의 손바닥이 어뢰를 툭 친다.

카메라 100mm 매크로, 락 오프.

빛 강한 작업등 하이라이트.

소리 딸깍, 띠 당김, 손바닥.

샷 0414–19초 · 5초 · THE SHIP LURCHES

구도 격납고 미디엄 와이드. 위에서 근탄 폭발. 작업등이 흔들리고 빈 운반대가 굴러간다.

  1. 14–15.5초 둔탁한 폭음과 함께 격납고가 기운다. 천장 먼지가 떨어진다.
  2. 15.5–17.5초 빈 운반대가 저절로 굴러가고 두 명이 몸으로 막는다.
  3. 17.5–19초 모두 한 번 위를 올려다본 뒤 바로 다시 일한다.

카메라 28mm 핸드헬드, 흔들림.

빛 흔들리는 빛, 먼지 줄기.

소리 폭음, 금속 신음, 굴러가는 바퀴, 위층 포성.

—

마지막 프레임 다시 일하는 손들.

샷 0519–26초 · 7초 · NOT DONE YET

구도 긴 대각선 와이드. 앞쪽 B5N은 어뢰, 뒤쪽은 아직 폭탄. 벽 옆 폭탄 더미. 땀에 젖은 작업원들.

  1. 19–21.5초 카메라가 줄을 따라 천천히 이동하며 바뀐 기체와 안 바뀐 기체를 지난다.
  2. 21.5–24초 반장이 땀을 닦고 위를 올려다본다. 위층 포성이 점점 거세진다.
  3. 24–26초 흔들리는 작업등이 화면을 가로질러 하얗게 덮는다(컷).

카메라 35mm, 줄을 따라 느린 측면 트래킹, 작업등 플레어로 끝.

빛 노란 작업등, 짙은 그림자, 땀.

소리 작업 소음, 커지는 위층 대공 사격.

좌→우.

마지막 프레임 작업등 플레어.

참고 @image1 · @image2 · @image3 · @image6

이 클립의 얼굴 고정 (인물 바이블 발췌)
  • 격납고 무장 반장 — 35세. 넓고 평평한 얼굴, 땀 맺힌 삭발, 굵은 목, 머리띠처럼 감은 수건, 목에 건 호루라기.
  • 격납고 무장 작업원들 — 18–25세 수병들. 삭발, 땀에 젖은 흰·회색 작업복, 면장갑, 머리띠·수건. 같은 얼굴 반복 금지.
동일 인물·무장·손상·공간 상태

빈 거치대에 어뢰 장착 준비. 한 기체에 두 무장 금지. 일부 기체는 아직 폭탄 상태. 벽 옆 폭탄 더미 유지.

전체 생성 프롬프트 펼치기 (6,709자)
[STYLE LOCK]
Photoreal live-action WWII epic, Battle of Midway, June 4 1942. Large-format cinema camera, spherical primes, fine film grain, soft highlight roll-off, deep atmospheric haze at distance.
Palette: steel-teal sea, milky high-key sky with towering cumulus, white coral sand, olive-drab, khaki and dark navy, warm tanned skin. Orange fire only at the instant of an explosion or a burning wing; flak is black, smoke charcoal, sea spray white. No oversaturated cyan, no game sheen.
Every frame in three layers: dark foreground occluder (sandbag, barrel, window frame, wing edge, hull rail) — subject — vast sky, sea or battle beyond. Exteriors: men small against the sky. Interiors: hard window light, haze, and the battle outside always visible or audible.
Real-time speed, no slow motion. 16:9, 1080p. Light, haze, smoke and wind stay continuous across cuts.

[TIME & PLACE]
Interior hangar: low work lamps, heat haze, sweat, swinging shadows; muffled AA above growing louder.

[IMAGES — all references, none as start/end frame]
@image1 [BG] Japanese carrier steel hangar: beams, passages, deck plates
@image2 [ASSET] B5N2 parked: wings spread, wheels down
@image3 [ASSET] B5N2 three-seat level bomber with land bomb
@image4 [ASSET] Japanese aerial torpedo on a wheeled cradle
@image5 [ASSET] Japanese ordnance carts, chocks, straps, tools
@image6 [ASSET] Japanese ordnance crew work clothes, cap, gloves

[REFERENCE LOCK]
BG images: copy layout, materials and geography only — not their camera, light or colour.
Aircraft/ship/weapon images: exact silhouette, structure, paint, markings, number of engines, barrels and funnels; never morph types.
Costume sheets: uniform and gear only, worn by living men with the CAST LOCK faces. Never show sheets, labels, mannequins or multiple views.
US insignia 1942: white star in blue circle, no red centre, no side bars. Japanese: red hinomaru.

[CAST LOCK — same faces in every shot]
ORDNANCE CHIEF — Japanese petty officer, 35. Broad flat face, shaved head glistening with sweat, thick neck, towel twisted into a headband, loud whistle on a cord.
ORDNANCE CREW — Japanese sailors, 18–25, shaved heads, white or grey work clothes darkened by sweat, cotton gloves, hachimaki or towels; every face different.
Japanese: authentic adult Japanese men of 1942 — lean, wiry, compact builds; enlisted heads shaved or near-shaved, officers short-cropped; monolid or hooded eyes, straight lower nose bridge, defined cheekbones, bronze tan, clean-shaven unless stated. Natural, dignified — never caricature, never modern idol styling.
Every other extra has a clearly different face; no clones.

[DIRECTION & PHYSICS]
American flight paths, ship headings, muzzles and profiles read RIGHT-TO-LEFT; Japanese read LEFT-TO-RIGHT — by camera placement, never by mirroring.
Propellers are blurred discs; aircraft keep exact shape; torpedoes fall nose-slightly-down, splash, then run straight leaving a bubble wake; bombs keep forward momentum; recoil, casings, spray and debris obey gravity. A destroyed aircraft never reappears. The camera never passes through glass, walls or hulls.

[NEGATIVE]
No on-screen text, subtitles, timestamps, logos, watermarks, letterbox. No music, no narration. No modern objects. No anime/CG/plastic look, no waxy skin, no cloned faces, no extra fingers or limbs.
One aircraft never carries a bomb and a torpedo at the same time. Torpedoes are never thrown or dropped on the deck.

[STRUCTURE]
26s, 5 shots, hard cuts. Timings are clip time.
First frame: hangar floor level: four men push a cradle carrying a long torpedo in from frame left; lamp light slides along its silver body.

[SHOTS]
Shot 1 (0–5s) — TORPEDO IN
Refs: @image4, @image1, @image6
Frame: Floor level. Foreground: a deck-plate seam. Midground: the torpedo cradle (@image4) and four men pushing. Background: a B5N2's empty rack.
Action:
- 0–2s: four men push the cradle; lamp light slides along the torpedo
- 2–3.5s: the front wheel catches on the plate seam; the cradle jerks
- 3.5–5s: they heave together; the wheel jumps the seam; the torpedo bounces once on its cradle; hands steady it at once
Camera: 24mm at floor level, tracking backward with the cradle.
Light: highlights on the torpedo body, sweat.
Sound: wheels, a thud, breathing, a wordless heave-call.
Ends on: the torpedo arriving under the empty rack.

Shot 2 (5–10s) · CUT TO — ALIGN
Refs: @image2, @image4, @image5, @image6
Frame: Under the B5N2's belly: one man guides the torpedo nose, another the tail fins.
Action:
- 5–7s: the jack lifts the torpedo slowly
- 7–9s: a tail fin nearly touches the fuselage; the rear man twists it true with his palm
- 9–10s: the torpedo stops just below the rack
Camera: 35mm low under the fuselage, a very slow tilt up with the lift.
Light: aircraft shadow, silver torpedo.
Sound: jack, metal brushing metal.
Ends on: the torpedo just under the rack.

Shot 3 (10–14s) · CUT TO — LOCK
Refs: @image5, @image2, @image6
Frame: Insert: the shackle closes, a strap is cinched; the chief's palm slaps the torpedo.
Action:
- 10–11.5s: the shackle snaps shut
- 11.5–13s: a strap is pulled tight twice
- 13–14s: the chief's palm slaps the torpedo
Camera: 100mm macro, locked-off.
Light: hard lamp highlights.
Sound: snap, strap, slap.
Ends on: the torpedo secured.

Shot 4 (14–19s) · CUT TO — THE SHIP LURCHES
Refs: @image1, @image6, @image5
Frame: Hangar medium-wide: a near miss above; lamps swing; an empty cart rolls.
Action:
- 14–15.5s: a dull blast; the hangar tilts; dust falls from the ceiling
- 15.5–17.5s: an empty cart rolls by itself; two men throw their bodies against it
- 17.5–19s: everyone looks up once, then goes straight back to work
Camera: 28mm handheld, shaken.
Light: swinging light, streams of dust.
Sound: the blast, groaning metal, rolling wheels, guns above.
Ends on: hands back at work.

Shot 5 (19–26s) · CUT TO — NOT DONE YET
Refs: @image1, @image2, @image3, @image6
Frame: Long diagonal wide: B5N2s in front now with torpedoes, those behind still with bombs; the bomb pile by the wall; sweat-soaked men.
Action:
- 19–21.5s: the camera glides along the row past converted and unconverted aircraft
- 21.5–24s: the chief wipes his sweat and looks up; the gunfire above grows heavier
- 24–26s: a swinging work lamp crosses the lens in a white flare — cut
Camera: 35mm slow lateral tracking along the row, ending on a lamp flare.
Light: yellow lamps, deep shadows, sweat.
Sound: work noise, AA overhead growing louder.
Ends on: the lamp flare.

[AUDIO]
Speak only the quoted lines, once each, in the stated language, at the stated moment. Diegetic sound only — wind, sea, engines, guns, metal, wordless voices — carried across cuts. No music, no narration.

C13 · 편집 위치 06:50–07:16

또 다른 구축함 · 다가오는 호넷 뇌격대

26초 · 5샷 · 첨부 7장 · 대사 0

다른 구축함 너머 수평선에 낮은 점들이 나타난다. 호넷 제8뇌격대의 TBD들이 바다에 붙어 느리게 다가온다. 일본 포수들이 실제로 조준하고 쏘며, 뇌격기들은 함선 사격을 그대로 맞는다.

프롬프트는 영어(시댄스용) · 샷 카드는 한국어(검토용) · 둘은 같은 내용

앞에서 받는 상황
C12의 거세지는 대공 사격 소리를 이어 받는다.

다음으로 넘기는 상황
한 대가 떨어져도 줄은 흐트러지지 않는다. 다음 컷 A 조종석.

첫 화면
다른 구축함 외경. 좌→우로 항진, 포들이 오른쪽을 겨눈다. 선수 너머 수평선에 낮은 점들.

샷 010–5초 · 5초 · ANOTHER DESTROYER

구도 수면 위 낮은 사선. 화면 왼쪽 3분의 2: 좌→우로 달리는 구축함(@image2), 선수 물보라. 오른쪽 수평선: 낮게 깔린 점들.

  1. 0–2.5초 구축함이 화면을 가로지르며 포들이 오른쪽으로 돈다.
  2. 2.5–5초 수평선 위 점들이 바다에 붙은 채 조금씩 커진다.

카메라 수면 위 2m, 구축함을 따라 느린 트래킹.

빛 반짝이는 해면, 회색 선체.

소리 선체가 파도를 가르는 소리, 먼 다수 엔진음, 먼 폭음.

구축함 좌→우 / 점들 우→좌.

샷 0415–20초 · 5초 · THEY SHOOT — Japanese gunners

구도 구축함 포좌. 대공포 사수가 조준석에서 포를 낮게 돌리고 급탄수가 탄창을 든다. 배경: 낮은 TBD 줄.

  1. 15–17초 사수가 포신을 수평 가까이 낮춘다.
  2. 17–19초 연사. 예광탄이 낮게 수평으로 뻗고 탄피가 쏟아진다.
  3. 19–20초 급탄수가 탄창을 내리꽂는다.

샷 0520–26초 · 6초 · FIRST LOSS

구도 TBD 대열 측면 중경. 대공 연기와 물기둥 사이.

  1. 20–22초 대공 연기가 대열 사이로 터지고 해면에 기관포탄 물보라가 줄을 긋는다.
  2. 22–24초 한 대(A·B 아님)의 날개가 터지며 불이 붙고 기수가 바다로 처진다.
  3. 24–26초 바다에 처박혀 물기둥이 솟는다. 나머지는 줄을 유지한 채 그 옆을 지나간다.

카메라 공중 측면 추적, 추락 지점에서 짧게 머묾.

빛 검은 연기, 흰 물, 주황 불꽃.

소리 폭발, 물 충돌음, 계속되는 엔진.

TBD 우→좌.

마지막 프레임 물기둥 옆을 지나는 대열.

이 클립의 얼굴 고정 (인물 바이블 발췌)
  • 아카기 견시원 — 30세. 사각의 그을린 얼굴, 미간의 깊은 세로 주름, 흰 모자 덮개 아래 삭발, 턱끈, 대형 거치 쌍안경.
  • 일본 대공포 사수 — 21세. 마른 얼굴, 날카로운 턱, 철모 아래 삭발, 악문 이, 흰 러닝셔츠. 연장 기관포 조준석.
  • 일본 대공포 급탄수 — 19세. 둥근 앳된 얼굴, 짧은 삭발, 넓은 미간, 상기된 볼, 목의 흰 수건. 15발 상자 탄창 장전.
동일 인물·무장·손상·공간 상태

호넷 제8뇌격대(TBD) — 엔터프라이즈를 호넷으로 표기하지 않는다. TBD 각 1발 어뢰. 이 클립에서 TBD 1대 추락(A·B 아님).

전체 생성 프롬프트 펼치기 (7,290자)
[STYLE LOCK]
Photoreal live-action WWII epic, Battle of Midway, June 4 1942. Large-format cinema camera, spherical primes, fine film grain, soft highlight roll-off, deep atmospheric haze at distance.
Palette: steel-teal sea, milky high-key sky with towering cumulus, white coral sand, olive-drab, khaki and dark navy, warm tanned skin. Orange fire only at the instant of an explosion or a burning wing; flak is black, smoke charcoal, sea spray white. No oversaturated cyan, no game sheen.
Every frame in three layers: dark foreground occluder (sandbag, barrel, window frame, wing edge, hull rail) — subject — vast sky, sea or battle beyond. Exteriors: men small against the sky. Interiors: hard window light, haze, and the battle outside always visible or audible.
Real-time speed, no slow motion. 16:9, 1080p. Light, haze, smoke and wind stay continuous across cuts.

[TIME & PLACE]
Late morning: high sun, sea glare, flak and white shell splashes over a low, slow line of aircraft.

[IMAGES — all references, none as start/end frame]
@image1 [BG] open Pacific surface, far horizon, broken cumulus
@image2 [ASSET] Kagero-class destroyer: hull, two funnels, gun mounts
@image3 [ASSET] carrier Akagi: hull, hinomaru on bow, island on PORT side, downward-curved funnel on STARBOARD side
@image4 [ASSET] TBD-1 Devastator: long tandem canopy, blue-grey, corrugated wings, main wheels half-protruding when retracted
@image5 [ASSET] Japanese twin 25mm AA mount: two parallel barrels, carriage, sights
@image6 [ASSET] Japanese AA crew uniform, helmet, magazine gear
@image7 [ASSET] US Mark 13 aerial torpedo: long body, nose, tail, mount

[REFERENCE LOCK]
BG images: copy layout, materials and geography only — not their camera, light or colour.
Aircraft/ship/weapon images: exact silhouette, structure, paint, markings, number of engines, barrels and funnels; never morph types.
Costume sheets: uniform and gear only, worn by living men with the CAST LOCK faces. Never show sheets, labels, mannequins or multiple views.
US insignia 1942: white star in blue circle, no red centre, no side bars. Japanese: red hinomaru.

[CAST LOCK — same faces in every shot]
AKAGI LOOKOUT — Japanese petty officer, 30. Square weathered face, deep vertical frown line between the brows, near-shaved head under a white cap cover, chin strap, large pedestal binoculars.
JAPANESE AA GUNNER — Japanese sailor, 21. Thin face, sharp jaw, shaved head under a steel helmet with chin strap, gritted teeth, white undershirt, sweat; sits in the pointer seat of a twin 25mm mount.
JAPANESE AA LOADER — Japanese sailor, 19. Round boyish face, near-shaved head, wide-set eyes, flushed cheeks, white towel around his neck, slams 15-round box magazines onto the gun.
Americans: varied European-American ancestry (Irish, Italian, German, Polish), varied hair colour, deeper-set eyes, higher nose bridges, forearm hair, sunburnt red-pink skin, stubble, sweat. Real 1942 faces — no movie-star polish.
Japanese: authentic adult Japanese men of 1942 — lean, wiry, compact builds; enlisted heads shaved or near-shaved, officers short-cropped; monolid or hooded eyes, straight lower nose bridge, defined cheekbones, bronze tan, clean-shaven unless stated. Natural, dignified — never caricature, never modern idol styling.
Every other extra has a clearly different face; no clones.

[DIRECTION & PHYSICS]
American flight paths, ship headings, muzzles and profiles read RIGHT-TO-LEFT; Japanese read LEFT-TO-RIGHT — by camera placement, never by mirroring.
Propellers are blurred discs; aircraft keep exact shape; torpedoes fall nose-slightly-down, splash, then run straight leaving a bubble wake; bombs keep forward momentum; recoil, casings, spray and debris obey gravity. A destroyed aircraft never reappears. The camera never passes through glass, walls or hulls.

[NEGATIVE]
No on-screen text, subtitles, timestamps, logos, watermarks, letterbox. No music, no narration. No modern objects. No anime/CG/plastic look, no waxy skin, no cloned faces, no extra fingers or limbs.
The TBDs fly very low (10–20 m) and close to the ships, not far away. Japanese guns are fired by visible men. Only one TBD (not A or B) crashes here.

[STRUCTURE]
26s, 5 shots, hard cuts. Timings are clip time.
First frame: a second destroyer steams left-to-right, guns trained right; beyond her bow, low specks on the horizon.

[SHOTS]
Shot 1 (0–5s) — ANOTHER DESTROYER
Refs: @image1, @image2
Frame: Low oblique over the water. Left two-thirds: a destroyer (@image2) running left-to-right, bow spray. Right horizon: low specks.
Action:
- 0–2.5s: the destroyer crosses frame; her guns swing right
- 2.5–5s: the specks on the horizon grow slowly, hugging the sea
Camera: 2 metres above the water, slow tracking with the destroyer.
Light: glittering sea, grey hull.
Sound: bow wave, distant many-engine drone, a far explosion.
Ends on: the specks on the horizon.

Shot 2 (5–10s) · CUT TO — LOOKOUT POV — torpedo bombers
Refs: @image1, @image4, @image2
Frame: The AKAGI LOOKOUT's binocular POV, twin-circle vignette: beyond the destroyers, a long line of TBDs 10–20 metres above the sea.
Action:
- 5–7s: the binoculars sweep and stop on the low line
- 7–10s: focus pulls in: about fifteen TBDs crawling toward them, glued to the sea
Camera: binocular POV, hand tremor.
Light: haze, low contrast.
Sound: wind, the lookout's breath.
Ends on: the low line spanning the frame.

Shot 3 (10–15s) · CUT TO — LOW AND SLOW — the Devastators
Refs: @image1, @image4, @image7, @image2
Frame: Side view over the sea. Foreground: the nearest TBD (@image4), torpedo (@image7) under its belly. Background: more TBDs, destroyers far off.
Action:
- 10–12.5s: the TBDs crawl right-to-left 15 metres above the sea; propwash ruffles the surface
- 12.5–15s: the first shell columns erupt ahead of them
Camera: aerial side tracking close to the water.
Light: glittering sea, blue-grey aircraft.
Sound: slow radial engines, wind, splashes.
Ends on: the line flying into the columns.

Shot 4 (15–20s) · CUT TO — THEY SHOOT — Japanese gunners
Refs: @image5, @image6, @image2
Frame: On the destroyer's mount: the AA GUNNER cranks the barrels low; the AA LOADER holds a magazine; the low TBD line behind.
Action:
- 15–17s: the gunner depresses the barrels almost level
- 17–19s: he fires; tracers stream out flat and low; brass pours
- 19–20s: the loader slams a magazine home
Camera: 40mm from behind the mount, over the barrels.
Light: muzzle flash, smoke.
Sound: twin bursts, brass.
Ends on: firing continuing.

Shot 5 (20–26s) · CUT TO — FIRST LOSS
Refs: @image1, @image4
Frame: Medium on the TBD line amid flak and shell splashes.
Action:
- 20–22s: flak bursts among the line; cannon splashes stitch the water
- 22–24s: one TBD (neither A nor B) has its wing burst into fire; its nose drops
- 24–26s: it slams into the sea in a column of water; the rest hold their line and fly past
Camera: aerial side tracking, a brief hold on the impact.
Light: black smoke, white water, orange flame.
Sound: blast, water impact, engines continuing.
Ends on: the line passing the column.

[AUDIO]
Speak only the quoted lines, once each, in the stated language, at the stated moment. Diegetic sound only — wind, sea, engines, guns, metal, wordless voices — carried across cuts. No music, no narration.

[REFERENCE LOCK]
BG images: copy layout, materials and geography only — not their camera, light or colour.
Aircraft/ship/weapon images: exact silhouette, structure, paint, markings, number of engines, barrels and funnels; never morph types.
Costume sheets: uniform and gear only, worn by living men with the CAST LOCK faces. Never show sheets, labels, mannequins or multiple views.
US insignia 1942: white star in blue circle, no red centre, no side bars. Japanese: red hinomaru.

[CAST LOCK — same faces in every shot]
TBD PILOT A — American Navy, 26. Square face, sandy-blond hair, blue eyes, strong square chin, clean-shaven, khaki flight suit, yellow life vest.
JAPANESE AA GUNNER — Japanese sailor, 21. Thin face, sharp jaw, shaved head under a steel helmet with chin strap, gritted teeth, white undershirt, sweat; sits in the pointer seat of a twin 25mm mount.
JAPANESE AA LOADER — Japanese sailor, 19. Round boyish face, near-shaved head, wide-set eyes, flushed cheeks, white towel around his neck, slams 15-round box magazines onto the gun.
Americans: varied European-American ancestry (Irish, Italian, German, Polish), varied hair colour, deeper-set eyes, higher nose bridges, forearm hair, sunburnt red-pink skin, stubble, sweat. Real 1942 faces — no movie-star polish.
Japanese: authentic adult Japanese men of 1942 — lean, wiry, compact builds; enlisted heads shaved or near-shaved, officers short-cropped; monolid or hooded eyes, straight lower nose bridge, defined cheekbones, bronze tan, clean-shaven unless stated. Natural, dignified — never caricature, never modern idol styling.

[DIRECTION & PHYSICS]
American flight paths, ship headings, muzzles and profiles read RIGHT-TO-LEFT; Japanese read LEFT-TO-RIGHT — by camera placement, never by mirroring.
Propellers are blurred discs; aircraft keep exact shape; torpedoes fall nose-slightly-down, splash, then run straight leaving a bubble wake; bombs keep forward momentum; recoil, casings, spray and debris obey gravity. A destroyed aircraft never reappears. The camera never passes through glass, walls or hulls.

[NEGATIVE]

Exactly one torpedo leaves TBD A and its rack is empty afterwards. No hit on the carrier. TBD A, once down, never reappears.

C15 · 편집 위치 07:46–08:12

“Zero incoming!”

26초 · 6샷 · 첨부 8장 · 대사 1

A가 사라진 거품 위로 무손상의 B가 들어온다. 캐노피 밖으로 함선 탄과 예광탄이 스치고, 위에서 새 엔진음이 내리꽂힌다. B 조종사가 외친다.

프롬프트는 영어(시댄스용) · 샷 카드는 한국어(검토용) · 둘은 같은 내용

앞에서 받는 상황
C14 끝 오른쪽에서 들려온 엔진음.

다음으로 넘기는 상황
제로센 두 대가 햇빛 속에서 내리꽂히기 시작하고, 후방 사수가 총을 위로 돌린다.

첫 화면
A의 거품이 남은 해면. 오른쪽에서 무손상 TBD B가 어뢰 한 발을 단 채 들어온다.

[STYLE LOCK]
Photoreal live-action WWII epic, Battle of Midway, June 4 1942. Large-format cinema camera, spherical primes, fine film grain, soft highlight roll-off, deep atmospheric haze at distance.
Palette: steel-teal sea, milky high-key sky with towering cumulus, white coral sand, olive-drab, khaki and dark navy, warm tanned skin. Orange fire only at the instant of an explosion or a burning wing; flak is black, smoke charcoal, sea spray white. No oversaturated cyan, no game sheen.
Every frame in three layers: dark foreground occluder (sandbag, barrel, window frame, wing edge, hull rail) — subject — vast sky, sea or battle beyond. Exteriors: men small against the sky. Interiors: hard window light, haze, and the battle outside always visible or audible.
Real-time speed, no slow motion. 16:9, 1080p. Light, haze, smoke and wind stay continuous across cuts.

[TIME & PLACE]
Late morning: high sun overhead (Zeros dive out of it), glare, flak.

- 24–26s: he swings the gun up at the diving Zeros
Camera: 50mm handheld, tilting up along the barrel.
Light: backlight, sweat.
Sound: the cocking clack, wind.
Ends on: the muzzle aimed at the sky.

[AUDIO]
Speak only the quoted lines, once each, in the stated language, at the stated moment. Diegetic sound only — wind, sea, engines, guns, metal, wordless voices — carried across cuts. No music, no narration.

C16 · 편집 위치 08:12–08:42

하강하는 제로센 · 바다로 떨어지는 뇌격대

30초 · 7샷 · 첨부 9장 · 대사 0

맞으면서도 뇌격기들은 어뢰를 떨어뜨린다. 그 위로 제로센들이 하강하며 쏘고, B는 좌현 날개 뿌리를 맞는다. 후방 사수의 응사도, 조종사의 마지막 회복 시도도 고도를 되돌리지 못한다.

프롬프트는 영어(시댄스용) · 샷 카드는 한국어(검토용) · 둘은 같은 내용

앞에서 받는 상황
C15의 위를 겨눈 총구와 급강하 엔진음.

다음으로 넘기는 상황
물속으로 가라앉는 B와 햇빛 줄기. 위에서 멀어지는 일본 엔진음. 1부 끝.

첫 화면
해면 위 낮은 와이드. 연기를 끄는 TBD 한 대가 가라앉으면서 어뢰를 떨어뜨리고, 앞쪽의 B도 투하 레버를 당긴다.

샷 010–5초 · 5초 · DROPPING WHILE DYING

구도 해면 위 낮은 와이드. 전경: B와 배 아래 어뢰. 중경: 연기를 끄는 다른 TBD. 배경: 아카기.

  1. 0–2초 연기를 끄는 TBD가 가라앉으면서도 어뢰를 떨어뜨리고, 어뢰가 물보라를 튀긴다.
  2. 2–3.5초 B의 어뢰가 배 아래에서 떨어져 얕게 입수한다.
  3. 3.5–5초 B가 빈 거치대를 보이며 계속 간다. 연기 나던 TBD는 바다에 닿는다.

카메라 해면 위 2m 측면 추적.

빛 반짝이는 해면, 검은 연기, 흰 물보라.

소리 엔진들, 입수, 충돌.

TBD 우→좌.

마지막 프레임 빈 거치대의 B.

샷 025–9초 · 4초 · THE DIVE

구도 위에서 아래로. 제로센 셋이 줄지어 하강, 아래 해면의 TBD들.

  1. 5–7초 제로센들이 연달아 내리꽂힌다.
  2. 7–9초 날개 기관포가 번쩍이고 해면에 탄착 물보라가 줄을 긋는다.

카메라 공중, 제로센 위 뒤쪽에서 추적.

빛 역광, 실루엣.

소리 급강하 고음, 기관포.

제로센 좌상→우하.

마지막 프레임 TBD 대열을 덮치는 제로센.

샷 039–12초 · 3초 · ZERO PILOT

구도 제로센 조종석, 제로센 조종사의 오른쪽 옆얼굴. 창밖 해면이 빠르게 흐른다.

  1. 9–10초 조종사가 조준기에 눈을 붙인다.
  2. 10–12초 짧게 사격한다. 눈과 조준기는 고정, 창밖만 흐른다.

연기 승리감 없이 일하듯.

카메라 85mm 측면, 진동.

마지막 프레임 감정 없는 눈.

샷 0412–16초 · 4초 · B IS HIT — left wing root

  1. 12–14초 두 기체가 교차하며 B의 좌현 날개 뿌리에 탄이 박혀 금속 조각이 튄다.
  2. 14–16초 날개가 처지고 불이 붙는다. 제로센은 충돌 없이 꼬리 쪽으로 멀어진다.

  1. 16–18초 사수가 짧게 연사한다. 예광탄이 제로센 쪽으로 뻗지만 기체 흔들림에 빗나간다.
  2. 18–20초 어깨로 총을 버티며 한 번 더 쏜다. 탄피가 바람에 날린다.

  2. 22–23.5초 잠깐 돌아온 수평선 위로 바다가 다시 차오른다.

샷 0725–30초 · 5초 · THE SEA TAKES HIM

구도 해면 높이 → 수중. B의 좌현 날개 끝이 물에 닿는다.

  1. 25–27초 좌현 날개가 물을 잡아채고 동체가 왼쪽으로 끌려 내려가며 낮은 물벽이 덮는다.
  2. 27–28초 불이 꺼지고 거품과 작은 파편만 남는다.
  3. 28–30초 수면 아래: 기포와 햇빛 줄기 속으로 B가 천천히 가라앉는다. 위에서 일본 엔진음이 멀어진다.

효과·물리 B는 다시 떠오르지 않는다.

카메라 해면 위 1m에서 수면 아래로 내려가는 하강 무브.

빛 흰 물 → 푸른 수중 빛줄기.

소리 충돌, 물 쏟아짐, 수중의 먹먹한 정적, 멀어지는 엔진.

B 우→좌로 끌려감.

마지막 프레임 햇빛 줄기 속으로 가라앉는 기체.

이 클립의 얼굴 고정 (인물 바이블 발췌)
  • TBD 조종사 B — 23세. 좁고 긴 얼굴, 짙은 갈색 웨이브, 처진 갈색 눈, 긴 콧대, 올리브 태닝.
  • TBD B 후방 사수 — 19세. 마른 체격, 튀어나온 귀, 짧은 쥐색 갈색 머리, 앳된 턱, 탄 코.
  • 제로센 조종사 — 24세. 둥근 얼굴, 굵은 일자 눈썹, 홑꺼풀, 왼쪽 턱의 작은 흰 흉터, 면도.
동일 인물·무장·손상·공간 상태

B: 어뢰 투하 → 빈 거치대 → 좌현 날개 뿌리 피탄 → 응사 → 회복 시도 → 추락. 항모 명중 없음. 일본 측 승리 표정·환호 없음.

전체 생성 프롬프트 펼치기 (8,285자)
[STYLE LOCK]
Photoreal live-action WWII epic, Battle of Midway, June 4 1942. Large-format cinema camera, spherical primes, fine film grain, soft highlight roll-off, deep atmospheric haze at distance.
Palette: steel-teal sea, milky high-key sky with towering cumulus, white coral sand, olive-drab, khaki and dark navy, warm tanned skin. Orange fire only at the instant of an explosion or a burning wing; flak is black, smoke charcoal, sea spray white. No oversaturated cyan, no game sheen.
Every frame in three layers: dark foreground occluder (sandbag, barrel, window frame, wing edge, hull rail) — subject — vast sky, sea or battle beyond. Exteriors: men small against the sky. Interiors: hard window light, haze, and the battle outside always visible or audible.
Real-time speed, no slow motion. 16:9, 1080p. Light, haze, smoke and wind stay continuous across cuts.

[TIME & PLACE]
Late morning: high sun, glare, flak; the final shot under the surface with blue light shafts.

[IMAGES — all references, none as start/end frame]
@image1 [BG] open Pacific surface, far horizon, broken cumulus
@image2 [ASSET] TBD-1 Devastator: long tandem canopy, blue-grey, corrugated wings, main wheels half-protruding when retracted
@image3 [ASSET] US Navy aircrew flight suit, helmet, yellow life vest
@image4 [ASSET] TBD front cockpit: narrow, gauges, canopy rails
@image5 [ASSET] US flexible rear machine gun: receiver, barrel, ammo feed, ring mount
@image6 [ASSET] A6M2 Zero, grey-green, red hinomaru, wheels retracted, no drop tank
@image7 [ASSET] Japanese naval aviator flight suit, leather helmet, goggles
@image8 [ASSET] US Mark 13 aerial torpedo: long body, nose, tail, mount
@image9 [ASSET] carrier Akagi: hull, hinomaru on bow, island on PORT side, downward-curved funnel on STARBOARD side

[REFERENCE LOCK]
BG images: copy layout, materials and geography only — not their camera, light or colour.
Aircraft/ship/weapon images: exact silhouette, structure, paint, markings, number of engines, barrels and funnels; never morph types.
Costume sheets: uniform and gear only, worn by living men with the CAST LOCK faces. Never show sheets, labels, mannequins or multiple views.
US insignia 1942: white star in blue circle, no red centre, no side bars. Japanese: red hinomaru.

[CAST LOCK — same faces in every shot]
TBD PILOT B — American Navy, 23. Long narrow face, dark-brown wavy hair, hooded brown eyes, long straight nose, olive tan, khaki flight suit, yellow life vest.
REAR GUNNER — American Navy, 19. Skinny, prominent ears, short mousy-brown hair, boyish chin, sunburnt nose, goggles on his helmet.
ZERO PILOT — Japanese naval aviator, 24. Rounder face, thick straight eyebrows, single-lid eyes, small pale scar on the left side of the chin, clean-shaven, leather helmet and goggles.
Americans: varied European-American ancestry (Irish, Italian, German, Polish), varied hair colour, deeper-set eyes, higher nose bridges, forearm hair, sunburnt red-pink skin, stubble, sweat. Real 1942 faces — no movie-star polish.
Japanese: authentic adult Japanese men of 1942 — lean, wiry, compact builds; enlisted heads shaved or near-shaved, officers short-cropped; monolid or hooded eyes, straight lower nose bridge, defined cheekbones, bronze tan, clean-shaven unless stated. Natural, dignified — never caricature, never modern idol styling.
Every other extra has a clearly different face; no clones.

[DIRECTION & PHYSICS]
American flight paths, ship headings, muzzles and profiles read RIGHT-TO-LEFT; Japanese read LEFT-TO-RIGHT — by camera placement, never by mirroring.
Propellers are blurred discs; aircraft keep exact shape; torpedoes fall nose-slightly-down, splash, then run straight leaving a bubble wake; bombs keep forward momentum; recoil, casings, spray and debris obey gravity. A destroyed aircraft never reappears. The camera never passes through glass, walls or hulls.

[NEGATIVE]
No on-screen text, subtitles, timestamps, logos, watermarks, letterbox. No music, no narration. No modern objects. No anime/CG/plastic look, no waxy skin, no cloned faces, no extra fingers or limbs.
B drops exactly one torpedo before it is hit; its rack stays empty. No Japanese victory smiles or cheering. No hit on the carrier.

[STRUCTURE]
30s, 7 shots, hard cuts. Timings are clip time.
First frame: low wide over the sea: a smoking TBD drops its torpedo while sinking lower; ahead of it, B pulls its release.

[SHOTS]
Shot 1 (0–5s) — DROPPING WHILE DYING
Refs: @image1, @image2, @image8, @image9
Frame: Low wide over the sea. Foreground: B with its torpedo. Midground: another TBD trailing smoke. Background: Akagi.
Action:
- 0–2s: the smoking TBD drops its torpedo even as it sinks lower; the torpedo splashes
- 2–3.5s: B's torpedo drops from its belly and enters the water shallow
- 3.5–5s: B flies on, rack empty; the smoking TBD touches the sea
Camera: side tracking 2 metres above the water.
Light: glittering sea, black smoke, white splashes.
Sound: engines, entry splashes, an impact.
Ends on: B with an empty rack.

Shot 2 (5–9s) · CUT TO — THE DIVE
Refs: @image6, @image2, @image1
Frame: Top to bottom: three Zeros diving in line onto the TBDs over the sea.
Action:
- 5–7s: the Zeros dive one after another
- 7–9s: wing guns flash; impact splashes stitch the sea
Camera: aerial, tracking from above and behind the Zeros.
Light: backlight, silhouettes.
Sound: diving whine, cannon.
Ends on: the Zeros falling on the line.

Shot 3 (9–12s) · CUT TO — ZERO PILOT
Refs: @image7, @image6
Frame: Zero cockpit: the ZERO PILOT in right profile; the sea streaming past outside.
Action:
- 9–10s: the pilot presses his eye to the sight
- 10–12s: he fires a short burst; eye and sight fixed, only the sea moves
Acting: no triumph — just work.
Camera: 85mm side profile, vibration.
Light: canopy-frame shadows, muzzle flash.
Sound: a short cannon burst.
Ends on: an emotionless eye.

Shot 4 (12–16s) · CUT TO — B IS HIT — left wing root
Refs: @image2, @image6, @image1
Frame: Crossing wide: the Zero above going left-to-right, B below going right-to-left.
Action:
- 12–14s: they cross; rounds punch into B's port wing root, metal flying
- 14–16s: the wing sags and catches fire; the Zero pulls away past B's tail without colliding
Camera: aerial side tracking, a short shake at the cross.
Light: orange flame, black smoke.
Sound: rounds punching metal, flame.
Ends on: the burning port wing.

Shot 5 (16–20s) · CUT TO — RETURN FIRE
Refs: @image5, @image3, @image6
Frame: Close on the REAR GUNNER; the Zero receding beyond the muzzle.
Action:
- 16–18s: he fires a short burst; tracers reach for the Zero but the shaking airframe throws them wide
- 18–20s: he braces with his shoulder and fires again; casings whip away in the wind
Camera: 50mm handheld, heavy shake.
Light: muzzle flash, smoke.
Sound: machine-gun bursts, wind.
Ends on: the Zero escaping.

Shot 6 (20–25s) · CUT TO — LAST FIGHT — the pilot
Refs: @image3, @image4, @image1
Frame: PILOT B, front three-quarter; the tilted horizon beyond the canopy.
Action:
- 20–22s: B hauls the stick to bring the tilted horizon back
- 22–23.5s: the horizon returns for a moment, then the sea rises again
- 23.5–25s: mouth shut, he holds on to the end
Camera: 50mm outside the canopy front, vibration.
Light: firelight on his face, smoke.
Sound: engine coughing, fire, wind.
Ends on: the sea filling the canopy.

Shot 7 (25–30s) · CUT TO — THE SEA TAKES HIM
Refs: @image2, @image1
Frame: Sea level, then under the surface: B's port wingtip touches the water.
Action:
- 25–27s: the port wing catches the water; the fuselage is dragged down left under a low wall of water
- 27–28s: the fire goes out; only foam and small debris remain
- 28–30s: under the surface: B sinks slowly through bubbles and blue light shafts; above, Japanese engines fade away
Camera: a descending move from 1 metre above the water to just under the surface.
Light: white water to blue underwater light shafts.
FX: B never resurfaces.
Sound: impact, water crashing, muffled underwater silence, fading engines.
Ends on: the aircraft sinking into the light shafts.
